## P4 - `--containers` flag silently ignored with the Clabernetes lab runtime

**Location:** [cmd/inspect.go:100-130](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/8828fb63101726c57131c104c9636f4e43ab259c/cmd/inspect.go#L100-L130)

**Problem:** The new `Inspect.Containers` option added in [`cmd/options.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/8828fb63101726c57131c104c9636f4e43ab259c/cmd/options.go#L1-L1) is only honored after the early return `if c.LabRuntime != nil { return c.ListLabRuntimeContainers(ctx, o.Destroy.All) }` at the top of `listContainers`. With `--runtime c9s`, the flag is silently ignored and the logical-node grouping is not applied. The new &#34;&#35;&#35;&#35;&#35; containers&#34; section in [`docs/cmd/inspect/index.md`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/8828fb63101726c57131c104c9636f4e43ab259c/docs/cmd/inspect/index.md#L1-L1) does not state that the flag is local-runtime-only.

**Trigger:** A user runs `containerlab inspect --runtime c9s --containers` against a Clabernetes-managed lab.

**Impact:** The flag silently does nothing for the Clabernetes runtime, which is confusing; at worst users expect a per-container listing and receive the default view with no indication why. Impact is low because Clabernetes labs presumably have no internal netns holders, making the logical view roughly a no-op there.

**Suggested correction:** Document in the &#34;&#35;&#35;&#35;&#35; containers&#34; section of [`docs/cmd/inspect/index.md`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/8828fb63101726c57131c104c9636f4e43ab259c/docs/cmd/inspect/index.md#L1-L1) that the flag applies to the local runtime only, or, if the lab-runtime listing can distinguish logical nodes, honor `Inspect.Containers` in `ListLabRuntimeContainers` as well. The documentation note is the minimal proportionate correction.


## P4 - Missing yaml-language-server schema header in [`tests/02-basic-srl/05-inspect-views.clab.yml`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/8828fb63101726c57131c104c9636f4e43ab259c/tests/02-basic-srl/05-inspect-views.clab.yml#L1-L1)

**Location:** [tests/02-basic-srl/05-inspect-views.clab.yml:1-1](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/8828fb63101726c57131c104c9636f4e43ab259c/tests/02-basic-srl/05-inspect-views.clab.yml#L1-L1)

**Problem:** The sibling new file [`tests/13-srsim/15-inspect-views.clab.yml`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/8828fb63101726c57131c104c9636f4e43ab259c/tests/13-srsim/15-inspect-views.clab.yml#L1-L1) begins with the `# yaml-language-server: $schema=...` comment consistent with the repository&#39;s existing test topologies, while [`tests/02-basic-srl/05-inspect-views.clab.yml`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/8828fb63101726c57131c104c9636f4e43ab259c/tests/02-basic-srl/05-inspect-views.clab.yml#L1-L1) omits it, making the two files introduced by this PR inconsistent.

**Trigger:** Opening the file in an editor with YAML schema validation, or comparing the two new test topologies added by this PR.

**Impact:** Cosmetic&#58; editors lose schema-based autocompletion and validation for this topology file.

**Suggested correction:** Add the same `# yaml-language-server: $schema=...` header line to [`tests/02-basic-srl/05-inspect-views.clab.yml`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/8828fb63101726c57131c104c9636f4e43ab259c/tests/02-basic-srl/05-inspect-views.clab.yml#L1-L1) to match the sibling file.


## Coverage

- Source-only review&#58; no tests were executed and no PR code was run; all conclusions are from static tracing of the head and base snapshots.
- All 15 changed paths were reviewed&#58; both workflow files, [`cmd/deploy.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/8828fb63101726c57131c104c9636f4e43ab259c/cmd/deploy.go#L1-L1), [`cmd/inspect.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/8828fb63101726c57131c104c9636f4e43ab259c/cmd/inspect.go#L1-L1), [`cmd/inspect_test.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/8828fb63101726c57131c104c9636f4e43ab259c/cmd/inspect_test.go#L1-L1), [`cmd/options.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/8828fb63101726c57131c104c9636f4e43ab259c/cmd/options.go#L1-L1), the two docs pages, the four new/modified Robot suites and resources, and [`tests/common.robot`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/8828fb63101726c57131c104c9636f4e43ab259c/tests/common.robot#L1-L1).
- `logicalNodeContainers` was traced against every unit-test vector in [`cmd/inspect_test.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/8828fb63101726c57131c104c9636f4e43ab259c/cmd/inspect_test.go#L1-L1) &#40;SR Linux with holder, distributed SR-SIM with CPM A, CPM-B-only fallback, internal tailscale node&#41; and against real label assignments in [`nodes/srl/netns.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/8828fb63101726c57131c104c9636f4e43ab259c/nodes/srl/netns.go#L1-L1), [`nodes/sros/netns.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/8828fb63101726c57131c104c9636f4e43ab259c/nodes/sros/netns.go#L1-L1), and [`nodes/sros/sros.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/8828fb63101726c57131c104c9636f4e43ab259c/nodes/sros/sros.go#L1-L1); logic is correct in all traced cases, including ext-containers passing through via the `key.node == ""` fallback.
- The `-t` filter &#40;keeping containers whose `NodeName` or `RootNodeName` is in `c.Nodes`&#41; correctly retains netns holders while dropping tool containers, matching the documented `--topo` behavior; Docker runtime name filter is exact-match anchored, so per-node listing returns one container per topology node.
- Deploy-summary correctness for distributed SR-SIM verified by tracing `c.deploy` -&#62; `c.finalize` -&#62; `ListNodesContainers`, which yields one container per topology node &#40;CPM A with holder-forged mgmt IPs&#41;; the Robot expectation `linux sr-a` is consistent.
- The relocated `Run Checked Pipeline` keyword in [`tests/common.robot`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/8828fb63101726c57131c104c9636f4e43ab259c/tests/common.robot#L1-L1) is safely resolved because [`tests/13-srsim/09-srsim-pause-container.robot`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/8828fb63101726c57131c104c9636f4e43ab259c/tests/13-srsim/09-srsim-pause-container.robot#L1-L1) imports `../common.robot`; root-level `.robot` files are an established resource convention and workflows invoke explicit suite paths.
- Minor unverified detail&#58; the Podman runtime&#39;s name filter semantics were assumed identical to the Docker runtime&#39;s exact-match filter; only the Docker implementation was fully read. The new suites&#39; `--all`/`-t` selectors do not depend on per-node name filtering.
- Performance note&#58; the `-t` path performs one extra label-based list call plus an O&#40;n&#42;m&#41; dedup merge versus base; negligible at realistic lab container counts and required by the new `--containers` design, so not raised as a finding.

<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

Clean up only your own mess.**\n\nWhen editing existing code:\n\n- Don't \"improve\" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.\n- Don't refactor things that aren't broken.\n- Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.\n- If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.\n\nWhen your changes create orphans:\n\n- Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.\n- Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.\n\nThe test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.\n\n## 4. Goal-Driven Execution\n\n**Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**\n\nTransform tasks into verifiable goals:\n\n- \"Add validation\" → \"Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass\"\n- \"Fix the bug\" → \"Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass\"\n- \"Refactor X\" → \"Ensure tests pass before and after\"\n\nFor multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:\n\n```\n1. [Step] → verify: [check]\n2. [Step] → verify: [check]\n3. [Step] → verify: [check]\n```\n\nStrong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria (\"make it work\") require constant clarification.\n\n---\n\n**These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.",

<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

254: +package cmd
255: +
256: +import (
257: +	"slices"
258: +	"testing"
259: +
260: +	clabconstants "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/constants"
261: +	clabruntime "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/runtime"
262: +)
263: +
264: +func TestLogicalNodeContainers(t *testing.T) {
265: +	ctr := func(lab, name, root string, internal bool, ip string) clabruntime.GenericContainer {
266: +		labels := map[string]string{
267: +			clabconstants.Containerlab: lab,
268: +			clabconstants.NodeName:     name,
269: +		}
270: +		if root != "" {
271: +			labels[clabconstants.RootNodeName] = root
272: +		}
273: +
274: +		if internal {
275: +			labels[clabconstants.InternalNode] = "true"
276: +		}
277: +
278: +		return clabruntime.GenericContainer{
279: +			Names:           []string{"clab-" + lab + "-" + name},
280: +			Labels:          labels,
281: +			NetworkSettings: clabruntime.GenericMgmtIPs{IPv4addr: ip},
282: +		}
283: +	}
284: +
285: +	tool := ctr("lab1", "", "", false, "10.0.0.9")
286: +	tool.Labels[clabconstants.ToolType] = "sshx"
287: +
288: +	got := logicalNodeContainers([]clabruntime.GenericContainer{
289: +		tool,
290: +		// internal node without a root node.
291: +		ctr("lab1", "tailscale", "", true, "10.0.0.8"),
292: +		ctr("lab1", "linux", "", false, "10.0.0.1"),
293: +		// SR Linux node with a netns holder.
294: +		ctr("lab1", "srl", "", false, ""),
295: +		ctr("lab1", "srl-netns", "srl", true, "10.0.0.2"),
296: +		// distributed SR-SIM node.
297: +		ctr("lab1", "sr-1", "sr", false, ""),
298: +		ctr("lab1", "sr-b", "sr", false, ""),
299: +		ctr("lab1", "sr-a", "sr", false, ""),
300: +		ctr("lab1", "sr-netns", "sr", true, "10.0.0.3"),
301: +		// distributed SR-SIM node without CPM A, and a node name shared with lab1.
302: +		ctr("lab2", "sr-1", "sr", false, ""),
303: +		ctr("lab2", "sr-b", "sr", false, ""),
304: +		ctr("lab2", "sr-netns", "sr", true, "10.0.0.4"),
305: +		ctr("lab2", "linux", "", false, "10.0.0.5"),
306: +	})
307: +
308: +	want := []string{
309: +		"clab-lab1-linux 10.0.0.1",
310: +		"clab-lab1-srl 10.0.0.2",
311: +		"clab-lab1-sr-a 10.0.0.3",
312: +		"clab-lab2-sr-b 10.0.0.4",
313: +		"clab-lab2-linux 10.0.0.5",
314: +	}
315: +
316: +	gotRows := make([]string, 0, len(got))
317: +	for idx := range got {
318: +		gotRows = append(gotRows, got[idx].Names[0]+" "+got[idx].NetworkSettings.IPv4addr)
319: +	}
320: +
321: +	if !slices.Equal(gotRows, want) {
322: +		t.Fatalf("logical node containers mismatch\n got: %v\nwant: %v", gotRows, want)
323: +	}
324: +}

341: diff --git a/docs/cmd/inspect/index.md b/docs/cmd/inspect/index.md
342: index 2fe51f6..2a5b391 100644
343: --- a/docs/cmd/inspect/index.md
344: +++ b/docs/cmd/inspect/index.md
345: @@ -65,10 +65,20 @@ The data is provided in the context of a container that belongs to a containerla
346:  
347:  The `inspect` command produces a brief summary about the running lab components. It is also possible to get a full view on the running containers by adding `--details` flag.
348:  
349:  With this flag inspect command will output every bit of information about the running containers. This is what `docker inspect` command provides.
350:  
351: +#### containers
352: +
353: +By default `inspect` shows one entry per topology node.
354: +
355: +Nodes that consist of several containers (ie. distributed SR-SIM) are represented by a single node that carries the management addresses of the node.
356: +
357: +The local flag `--containers` will list every container of a lab rather than just the logical nodes.
358: +
359: +When the lab is selected with the `--topo` flag, only the containers of the nodes defined in that topology file are listed.
360: +
361:  #### wide
362:  
363:  The local `-w | --wide` flag adds all available columns to the `inspect` output table.
364:  
365:  ### Examples
366: diff --git a/docs/manual/kinds/srl.md b/docs/manual/kinds/srl.md
367: index 269d4ba..3aac436 100644
368: --- a/docs/manual/kinds/srl.md
369: +++ b/docs/manual/kinds/srl.md
370: @@ -468,11 +468,11 @@ The license file lifts these limitations as well as unlocks chassis-based platfo
371:  
372:  ### Reboot
373:  
374:  A reboot issued from within the node, for example with `tools platform chassis reboot`, restarts the SR Linux container. The node comes back with its links, saved configuration and management address intact.
375:  
376: -To support that, SR Linux nodes default to the `unless-stopped` [restart policy](../nodes.md#restart-policy), and containerlab keeps the links and the management address of each node in an internal `<node-name>-netns` container. The internal container is only listed by `containerlab inspect --all`, and it is removed together with the node.
377: +To support that, SR Linux nodes default to the `unless-stopped` [restart policy](../nodes.md#restart-policy), and containerlab keeps the links and the management address of each node in an internal `<node-name>-netns` container. The internal container is only listed by `containerlab inspect` with the [`--containers`](../../cmd/inspect/index.md#containers) or `--details` flag, and it is removed together with the node.
378:  
379:  Use `containerlab stop`, `start` and `restart` to manage the lifecycle of SR Linux nodes instead of restarting the containers with the container runtime directly.
380:  
381:  Nodes with a user-defined [`network-mode`](../nodes.md#network-mode) do not use the internal container.

111: func inspectFn(cobraCmd *cobra.Command, o *Options) error {
112: 	if o.Global.TopologyName == "" && o.Global.TopologyFile == "" && !o.Destroy.All {
113: 		return fmt.Errorf(
114: 			"provide either a lab name (--name) or a topology file path (--topo) or the --all flag",
115: 		)
116: 	}
117: 
118: 	// Format validation (only relevant if --details is NOT used)
119: 	if !o.Inspect.Details &&
120: 		o.Inspect.Format != clabconstants.FormatTable &&
121: 		o.Inspect.Format != clabconstants.FormatJSON &&
122: 		o.Inspect.Format != clabconstants.FormatCSV {
123: 		return fmt.Errorf(
124: 			"output format %q is not supported when --details is not used, use "+
125: 				"'table', 'json' or 'csv'",
126: 			o.Inspect.Format,
127: 		)
128: 	}
129: 	// If --details is used, the format is implicitly JSON.
130: 	if o.Inspect.Details {
131: 		o.Inspect.Format = clabconstants.FormatJSON // Force JSON format if details are requested
132: 	}
133: 
134: 	c, err := clabcore.NewContainerLab(o.ToClabOptions()...)
135: 	if err != nil {
136: 		return err
137: 	}
138: 
139: 	containers, err := listContainers(cobraCmd.Context(), c, o)
140: 	if err != nil {
141: 		return err
142: 	}
143: 
144: 	// Handle empty results
145: 	if len(containers) == 0 {
146: 		switch o.Inspect.Format {
147: 		case clabconstants.FormatJSON:
148: 			fmt.Println("{}")
149: 		case clabconstants.FormatCSV:
150: 			fmt.Println(
151: 				"lab_name,labPath,absLabPath,name,container_id,image,kind,state,status," +
152: 					"ipv4_address,ipv6_address,owner",
153: 			)
154: 		default: // Table format
155: 			log.Info("no containers found")
156: 		}
157: 
158: 		return err
159: 	}
160: 
161: 	// Handle --details (always produces grouped JSON output)
162: 	if o.Inspect.Details {
163: 		return printContainerDetailsJSON(containers)
164: 	}
165: 
166: 	// Handle non-details cases (table or grouped JSON summary)
167: 	err = PrintContainerInspect(containers, o)
168: 
169: 	return err
170: }
171: 
172: // listContainers handles listing containers based on different criteria (topology or labels).
173: func listContainers(
174: 	ctx context.Context,
175: 	c *clabcore.CLab,
176: 	o *Options,
177: ) ([]clabruntime.GenericContainer, error) {
178: 	if c.LabRuntime != nil {
179: 		return c.ListLabRuntimeContainers(ctx, o.Destroy.All)
180: 	}
181: 
182: 	var listOptions []clabcore.ListOption
183: 
184: 	switch {
185: 	case o.Global.TopologyFile != "":
186: 		listOptions = append(listOptions, clabcore.WithListLabName(c.Config.Name))
187: 	case o.Global.TopologyName != "":
188: 		listOptions = append(listOptions, clabcore.WithListLabName(o.Global.TopologyName))
189: 	default:
190: 		listOptions = append(listOptions, clabcore.WithListclabLabelExists())
191: 	}
192: 
193: 	containers, err := c.ListContainers(ctx, listOptions...)
194: 	if err != nil {
195: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to list containers based on labels: %s", err)
196: 	}
197: 
198: 	if o.Global.TopologyFile != "" {
199: 		containers = slices.DeleteFunc(containers, func(ctr clabruntime.GenericContainer) bool {
200: 			_, isNode := c.Nodes[ctr.Labels[clabconstants.NodeName]]
201: 			_, isComponent := c.Nodes[ctr.Labels[clabconstants.RootNodeName]]
202: 
203: 			return !isNode && !isComponent
204: 		})
205: 
206: 		// containers that containerlab did not create (ie. ext-container) have no lab labels.
207: 		nodeContainers, err := c.ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound(ctx)
208: 		if err != nil {
209: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to list containers based on topology: %s", err)
210: 		}
211: 
212: 		for idx := range nodeContainers {
213: 			if !slices.ContainsFunc(containers, func(ctr clabruntime.GenericContainer) bool {
214: 				return ctr.ID == nodeContainers[idx].ID
215: 			}) {
216: 				containers = append(containers, nodeContainers[idx])
217: 			}
218: 		}
219: 	}
220: 
221: 	// --details always describes every container of a lab.
222: 	if o.Inspect.Containers || o.Inspect.Details {
223: 		return containers, nil
224: 	}
225: 
226: 	return logicalNodeContainers(containers), nil
227: }
228: 
229: // logicalNodeContainers returns a single logical topology node.
230: func logicalNodeContainers(
231: 	containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer,
232: ) []clabruntime.GenericContainer {
233: 	// Node names are unique only within a lab.
234: 	type nodeKey struct{ lab, node string }
235: 
236: 	holders := make(map[nodeKey]*clabruntime.GenericContainer)
237: 	hasCPMA := make(map[nodeKey]bool)
238: 
239: 	for idx := range containers {
240: 		l := containers[idx].Labels
241: 		key := nodeKey{l[clabconstants.Containerlab], l[clabconstants.RootNodeName]}
242: 
243: 		switch {
244: 		case key.node == "":
245: 		case l[clabconstants.InternalNode] == "true":
246: 			holders[key] = &containers[idx]
247: 		case l[clabconstants.NodeName] == key.node+"-a":
248: 			hasCPMA[key] = true
249: 		}
250: 	}
251: 
252: 	result := make([]clabruntime.GenericContainer, 0, len(containers))
253: 
254: 	for idx := range containers {
255: 		ctr := containers[idx]
256: 		name := ctr.Labels[clabconstants.NodeName]
257: 		key := nodeKey{
258: 			ctr.Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab],
259: 			ctr.Labels[clabconstants.RootNodeName],
260: 		}
261: 
262: 		// Components are named <root node>-<lowercase slot>.
263: 		isCPM := name == key.node+"-a" || (name == key.node+"-b" && !hasCPMA[key])
264: 		if ctr.Labels[clabconstants.ToolType] != "" ||
265: 			ctr.Labels[clabconstants.InternalNode] == "true" || (key.node != "" && !isCPM) {
266: 			continue
267: 		}
268: 
269: 		if key.node == "" {
270: 			key.node = name
271: 		}
272: 
273: 		if holder := holders[key]; holder != nil {
274: 			ctr.NetworkName = holder.NetworkName
275: 			ctr.NetworkSettings = holder.NetworkSettings
276: 			ctr.Ports = holder.Ports
277: 		}
278: 
279: 		result = append(result, ctr)
280: 	}
281: 
282: 	return result
283: }
284: 
285: func toTableData(contDetails []clabtypes.ContainerDetails, o *Options) []tableWriter.Row {
286: 	tabData := make([]tableWriter.Row, 0, len(contDetails))
287: 
288: 	for i := range contDetails {
289: 		d := &contDetails[i]
290: 		tabRow := tableWriter.Row{}
291: 
292: 		if o.Destroy.All {
293: 			tabRow = append(tabRow, d.LabPath, d.LabName)
294: 		}
295: 
296: 		if o.Inspect.Wide {
297: 			tabRow = append(tabRow, d.Owner)
298: 		}
299: 
300: 		// we do not want to print status other than health in the table view
301: 		if !strings.Contains(d.Status, "health") {
302: 			d.Status = ""
303: 		} else {
304: 			d.Status = fmt.Sprintf("(%s)", d.Status)
305: 		}
306: 
307: 		// Common fields
308: 		if o.Inspect.Wide {
309: 			// Print all fields on one line, no newlines
310: 			tabRow = append(tabRow,
311: 				d.Name,
312: 				fmt.Sprintf("%s %s", d.Kind, d.Image),
313: 				fmt.Sprintf("%s %s", d.State, d.Status),
314: 				fmt.Sprintf("%s %s",
315: 					ipWithoutPrefix(d.IPv4Address),
316: 					ipWithoutPrefix(d.IPv6Address)))
317: 		} else {
318: 			tabRow = append(tabRow,
319: 				d.Name,
320: 				fmt.Sprintf("%s\n%s", d.Kind, d.Image),
321: 				fmt.Sprintf("%s\n%s", d.State, d.Status),
322: 				fmt.Sprintf("%s\n%s",
323: 					ipWithoutPrefix(d.IPv4Address),
324: 					ipWithoutPrefix(d.IPv6Address)))
325: 		}
326: 
327: 		tabData = append(tabData, tabRow)
328: 	}
329: 
330: 	return tabData
331: }
332: 
333: // getShortestTopologyPath calculates the relative path to the provided topology file from
334: // the current working directory and returns it if it is shorted than the absolute path p.
335: func getShortestTopologyPath(p string) (string, error) {
336: 	if p == "" {
337: 		return "", nil
338: 	}
339: 	if strings.Contains(p, "://") {
340: 		return p, nil
341: 	}
342: 
343: 	// get topo file path relative of the cwd
344: 	cwd, err := os.Getwd()
345: 	if err != nil {
346: 		return "", err
347: 	}
348: 
349: 	relPath, err := filepath.Rel(cwd, p)
350: 	if err != nil {
351: 		return "", err
352: 	}
353: 
354: 	if len(relPath) < len(p) {
355: 		return relPath, nil
356: 	}
357: 
358: 	return p, nil
359: }
360: 
361: // printContainerDetailsJSON handles the detailed JSON output of containers grouped by lab name.
362: func printContainerDetailsJSON(containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer) error {
363: 	groupedDetails := make(map[string][]clabruntime.GenericContainer)
364: 	// Sort containers first by lab name, then by container name for consistent output
365: 	sort.Slice(containers, func(i, j int) bool {
366: 		labNameI := containers[i].Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab]
367: 		labNameJ := containers[j].Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab]
368: 
369: 		if labNameI == labNameJ {
370: 			// Use the first name if available
371: 			nameI := ""
372: 			if len(containers[i].Names) > 0 {
373: 				nameI = containers[i].Names[0]
374: 			}
375: 
376: 			nameJ := ""
377: 			if len(containers[j].Names) > 0 {
378: 				nameJ = containers[j].Names[0]
379: 			}
380: 
381: 			return nameI < nameJ
382: 		}
383: 
384: 		return labNameI < labNameJ
385: 	})
386: 
387: 	// Group the sorted containers
388: 	for idx := range containers {
389: 		labName := containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab]
390: 		// Ensure labName exists, default to a placeholder if missing
391: 		// (shouldn't happen with filters)
392: 		if labName == "" {
393: 			labName = "_unknown_lab_"
394: 		}
395: 
396: 		groupedDetails[labName] = append(groupedDetails[labName], containers[idx])
397: 	}
398: 
399: 	b, err := json.MarshalIndent(groupedDetails, "", "  ")
400: 	if err != nil {
401: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal grouped container details: %v", err)
402: 	}
403: 
404: 	fmt.Println(string(b))
405: 
406: 	return nil
407: }
408: 
409: func printContainerInspectJSON(contDetails []clabtypes.ContainerDetails) error {
410: 	// Group summary results by LabName
411: 	// Use a map where keys are lab names and values are slices of container details
412: 	groupedLabs := make(map[string][]clabtypes.ContainerDetails)
413: 
414: 	for idx := range contDetails {
415: 		labName := contDetails[idx].LabName
416: 		if labName == "" {
417: 			labName = "_unknown_lab_" // Should not happen if filters work correctly
418: 		}
419: 		// Assign the *entire* ContainerDetails struct (including AbsLabPath)
420: 		groupedLabs[labName] = append(groupedLabs[labName], contDetails[idx])
421: 	}
422: 
423: 	b, err := json.MarshalIndent(groupedLabs, "", "  ")
424: 	if err != nil {
425: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal grouped container summary: %v", err)
426: 	}
427: 
428: 	fmt.Println(string(b))
429: 
430: 	return nil
431: }
432: 
433: func printContainerInspectTable(contDetails []clabtypes.ContainerDetails, o *Options) {
434: 	// Generate and render table using the summary data (which uses relative LabPath)
435: 	tabData := toTableData(contDetails, o)
436: 	table := tableWriter.NewWriter()
437: 	table.SetOutputMirror(os.Stdout)
438: 	table.SetStyle(tableWriter.StyleRounded)
439: 	table.Style().Format.Header = text.FormatTitle
440: 	table.Style().Format.HeaderAlign = text.AlignCenter
441: 	table.Style().Options.SeparateRows = true
442: 	table.Style().Color = tableWriter.ColorOptions{
443: 		Header: text.Colors{text.Bold},
444: 	}
445: 
446: 	headerBase := tableWriter.Row{"Name", "Kind/Image", "State", "IPv4/6 Address"}
447: 	if o.Inspect.Wide {
448: 		headerBase = slices.Insert(headerBase, 0, "Owner")
449: 	}
450: 
451: 	var header tableWriter.Row
452: 
453: 	var colConfigs []tableWriter.ColumnConfig
454: 
455: 	// Lab-level columns (Topology, Lab Name, Owner) AutoMerge across nodes of the same lab.
456: 	if o.Destroy.All {
457: 		header = append(tableWriter.Row{"Topology", "Lab Name"}, headerBase...)
458: 		colConfigs = append(
459: 			colConfigs,
460: 			tableWriter.ColumnConfig{
461: 				Number:    1,
462: 				AutoMerge: true, VAlign: text.VAlignMiddle,
463: 			},
464: 			tableWriter.ColumnConfig{
465: 				Number:    2, //nolint: mnd
466: 				AutoMerge: true, VAlign: text.VAlignMiddle,
467: 			},
468: 		)
469: 		if o.Inspect.Wide {
470: 			colConfigs = append(colConfigs, tableWriter.ColumnConfig{
471: 				Number:    3, //nolint: mnd
472: 				AutoMerge: true, VAlign: text.VAlignMiddle,
473: 			})
474: 		}
475: 	} else {
476: 		header = headerBase
477: 
478: 		colConfigs = append(colConfigs, tableWriter.ColumnConfig{
479: 			Number:    1,
480: 			AutoMerge: true, VAlign: text.VAlignMiddle,
481: 		})
482: 	}
483: 
484: 	table.AppendHeader(header)
485: 
486: 	if len(colConfigs) > 0 {
487: 		table.SetColumnConfigs(colConfigs)
488: 	}
489: 
490: 	table.AppendRows(tabData)
491: 	table.Render()
492: }
493: 
494: func printContainerInspectCSV(contDetails []clabtypes.ContainerDetails) {
495: 	csv := "lab_name,labPath,absLabPath,name,container_id,image,kind,state," +
496: 		"status,ipv4_address,ipv6_address,owner\n"
497: 	for idx := range contDetails {
498: 		csv += fmt.Sprintf("%v,%v,%v,%v,%v,%v,%v,%v,%v,%v,%v,%v\n",
499: 			contDetails[idx].LabName,
500: 			contDetails[idx].LabPath,
501: 			contDetails[idx].AbsLabPath,
502: 			contDetails[idx].Name,
503: 			contDetails[idx].ContainerID,
504: 			contDetails[idx].Image,
505: 			contDetails[idx].Kind,
506: 			contDetails[idx].State,
507: 			contDetails[idx].Status,
508: 			contDetails[idx].IPv4Address,
509: 			contDetails[idx].IPv6Address,
510: 			contDetails[idx].Owner)
511: 	}
512: 
513: 	fmt.Print(csv)
514: }
515: 
516: // PrintContainerInspect handles non-details output (table or grouped JSON summary).
517: func PrintContainerInspect(containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer, o *Options) error {
518: 	contDetails := make([]clabtypes.ContainerDetails, 0, len(containers))
519: 
520: 	// Gather summary details of each container
521: 	for idx := range containers {
522: 		absPath := containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.TopoFile]
523: 
524: 		shortPath, err := getShortestTopologyPath(absPath)
525: 		if err != nil {
526: 			log.Warnf(
527: 				"failed to get relative topology path for container %s: %v, using raw path %q",
528: 				containers[idx].Names[0],
529: 				err,
530: 				absPath,
531: 			)
532: 
533: 			shortPath = absPath // Use raw path as fallback for display
534: 		}
535: 
536: 		status := parseStatus(containers[idx].Status)
537: 
538: 		cdet := clabtypes.ContainerDetails{
539: 			LabName:     containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab],
540: 			LabPath:     shortPath, // Relative or shortest path for table view
541: 			AbsLabPath:  absPath,   // Absolute path for JSON view
542: 			Image:       containers[idx].Image,
543: 			State:       containers[idx].State,
544: 			Status:      status,
545: 			IPv4Address: containers[idx].GetContainerIPv4(),
546: 			IPv6Address: containers[idx].GetContainerIPv6(),
547: 			ContainerID: containers[idx].ShortID,
548: 			// Only lab runtimes set the workload; it adds namespace, pod, container, node_uid,
549: 			// exposed_ports and load_balancer_address to the JSON output.
550: 			LabRuntimeWorkload: containers[idx].Workload,
551: 		}
552: 
553: 		if len(containers[idx].Names) > 0 {
554: 			cdet.Name = containers[idx].Names[0]
555: 		}
556: 
557: 		if group, ok := containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.NodeGroup]; ok {
558: 			cdet.Group = group
559: 		}
560: 
561: 		if kind, ok := containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.NodeKind]; ok {
562: 			cdet.Kind = kind
563: 		}
564: 
565: 		if owner, ok := containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.Owner]; ok {
566: 			cdet.Owner = owner
567: 		}
568: 
569: 		contDetails = append(contDetails, cdet)
570: 	}
571: 
572: 	// Sort summary details by lab name, then container name
573: 	sort.Slice(contDetails, func(i, j int) bool {
574: 		if contDetails[i].LabName == contDetails[j].LabName {
575: 			return contDetails[i].Name < contDetails[j].Name
576: 		}
577: 
578: 		return contDetails[i].LabName < contDetails[j].LabName
579: 	})
580: 
581: 	switch o.Inspect.Format {
582: 	case clabconstants.FormatJSON:
583: 		err := printContainerInspectJSON(contDetails)
584: 		if err != nil {
585: 			return err
586: 		}
587: 	case "table":
588: 		printContainerInspectTable(contDetails, o)
589: 	case "csv":
590: 		printContainerInspectCSV(contDetails)
591: 	default:
592: 		return fmt.Errorf("internal error: unhandled format %q", o.Inspect.Format)
593: 	}
594: 
595: 	return nil
596: }
597: 
598: // parseStatus extracts a simpler status string, focusing on health states.
599: func parseStatus(status string) string {
600: 	switch {
601: 	case strings.Contains(status, "unhealthy"):
602: 		return "unhealthy"
603: 	case strings.Contains(status, "health: starting"):
604: 		return "health: starting"
605: 	case strings.Contains(status, "healthy"):
606: 		return "healthy"
607: 	default:
608: 		// Return original status if no specific health info found
609: 		return status
610: 	}
611: }
612: 
613: // ipWithoutPrefix removes the CIDR prefix length from an IP address string.
614: // Returns "N/A" if input contains "N/A".
615: // Returns original string if it doesn't contain exactly one "/".
616: func ipWithoutPrefix(ip string) string {
617: 	if strings.Contains(ip, clabconstants.NotApplicable) {
618: 		return ip
619: 	}
620: 
621: 	ipParts := strings.Split(ip, "/")
622: 	if len(ipParts) != 2 { //nolint: mnd
623: 		return ip // Return original if not in expected format "address/prefix"
624: 	}
625: 
626: 	return ipParts[0]
627: }

(End of file - total 627 lines)

PrintContainerInspect(containers, o)\n\n\treturn err\n}\n\n// listContainers handles listing containers based on different criteria (topology or labels).\nfunc listContainers(\n\tctx context.Context,\n\tc *clabcore.CLab,\n\to *Options,\n) ([]clabruntime.GenericContainer, error) {\n\tif c.LabRuntime != nil {\n\t\treturn c.ListLabRuntimeContainers(ctx, o.Destroy.All)\n\t}\n\n\tvar node.\nfunc logicalNodeContainers(\n\tcontainers []clabruntime.GenericContainer,\n) []clabruntime.GenericContainer {\n\t// Node names are unique only within a lab.\n\ttype nodeKey struct{ lab, node string }\n\n\tholders := make(map[nodeKey]*clabruntime.GenericContainer)\n\thasCPMA := make(map[nodeKey]bool)\n\n\tfor idx := range containers {\n\t\tl := containers[idx].Labels\n\t\tkey := nodeKey{l[clabconstants.Containerlab], l[clabconstants.RootNodeName]}\n\n\t\tswitch {\n\t\tcase key.node == \"\":\n\t\tcase l[clabconstants.InternalNode] == \"true\":\n\t\t\tholders[key] = &containers[idx]\n\t\tcase l[clabconstants.NodeName] == key.node+\"-a\":\n\t\t\thasCPMA[key] = true\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tresult := make([]clabruntime.GenericContainer, 0, len(containers))\n\n\tfor idx := range containers {\n\t\tctr := containers[idx]\n\t\tname := ctr.Labels[clabconstants.NodeName]\n\t\tkey := nodeKey{\n\t\t\tctr.Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab],\n\t\t\tctr.Labels[clabconstants.RootNodeName],\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Components are named <root node>-<lowercase slot>.\n\t\tisCPM := name == key.node+\"-a\" || (name == key.node+\"-b\" && !hasCPMA[key])\n\t\tif ctr.Labels[clabconstants.ToolType] != \"\" ||\n\t\t\tctr.Labels[clabconstants.InternalNode] == \"true\" || (key.node != \"\" && !isCPM) {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif key.node == \"\" {\n\t\t\tkey.node = name\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif holder := holders[key]; holder != nil {\n\t\t\tctr.NetworkName = holder.NetworkName\n\t\t\tctr.NetworkSettings = holder.NetworkSettings\n\t\t\tctr.Ports = holder.Ports\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tresult = append(result, ctr)\n\t}\n\n\treturn result\n}\n\nfunc toTableData(contDetails []clabtypes.ContainerDetails, o *Options) []tableWriter.Row {\n\ttabData := make([]tableWriter.Row, 0, len(contDetails))\n\n\tfor i := range contDetails {\n\t\td := &contDetails[i]\n\t\ttabRow := tableWriter.Row{}\n\n\t\tif o.Destroy.All {\n\t\t\ttabRow = append(tabRow, d.LabPath, d.LabName)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif o.Inspect.Wide {\n\t\t\ttabRow = append(tabRow, d.Owner)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// we do not want to print status other than health in the table view\n\t\tif !strings.Contains(d.Status, \"health\") {\n\t\t\td.Status = \"\"\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\td.Status = fmt.Sprintf(\"(%s)\", d.Status)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Common fields\n\t\tif o.Inspect.Wide {\n\t\t\t// Print all fields on one line, no newlines\n\t\t\ttabRow = append(tabRow,\n\t\t\t\td.Name,\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Sprintf(\"%s %s\", d.Kind, d.Image),\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Sprintf(\"%s %s\", d.State, d.Status),\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Sprintf(\"%s %s\",\n\t\t\t\t\tipWithoutPrefix(d.IPv4Address),\n\t\t\t\t\tipWithoutPrefix(d.IPv6Address)))\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\ttabRow = append(tabRow,\n\t\t\t\td.Name,\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Sprintf(\"%s\\n%s\", d.Kind, d.Image),\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Sprintf(\"%s\\n%s\", d.State, d.Status),\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Sprintf(\"%s\\n%s\",\n\t\t\t\t\tipWithoutPrefix(d.IPv4Address),\n\t\t\t\t\tipWithoutPrefix(d.IPv6Address)))\n\t\t}\n\n\t\ttabData = append(tabData, tabRow)\n\t}\n\n\treturn tabData\n}\n\n// getShortestTopologyPath calculates the relative path to the provided topology file from\n// the current working directory and returns it if it is shorted than the absolute path p.\nfunc getShortestTopologyPath(p string) (string, error) {\n\tif p == \"\" {\n\t\treturn \"\", nil\n\t}\n\tif strings.Contains(p, \"://\") {\n\t\treturn p, nil\n\t}\n\n\t// get topo file path relative of the cwd\n\tcwd, err := os.Getwd()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn \"\", err\n\t}\n\n\trelPath, err := filepath.Rel(cwd, p)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn \"\", err\n\t}\n\n\tif len(relPath) < len(p) {\n\t\treturn relPath, nil\n\t}\n\n\treturn p, nil\n}\n\n// printContainerDetailsJSON handles the detailed JSON output of containers grouped by lab name.\nfunc printContainerDetailsJSON(containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer) error {\n\tgroupedDetails := make(map[string][]clabruntime.GenericContainer)\n\t// Sort containers first by lab name, then by container name for consistent output\n\tsort.Slice(containers, func(i, j int) bool {\n\t\tlabNameI := containers[i].Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab]\n\t\tlabNameJ := containers[j].Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab]\n\n\t\tif labNameI == labNameJ {\n\t\t\t// Use the first name if available\n\t\t\tnameI := \"\"\n\t\t\tif len(containers[i].Names) > 0 {\n\t\t\t\tnameI = containers[i].Names[0]\n\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\tnameJ := \"\"\n\t\t\tif len(containers[j].Names) > 0 {\n\t\t\t\tnameJ = containers[j].Names[0]\n\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\treturn nameI < nameJ\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn labNameI < labNameJ\n\t})\n\n\t// Group the sorted containers\n\tfor idx := range containers {\n\t\tlabName := containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab]\n\t\t// Ensure labName exists, default to a placeholder if missing\n\t\t// (shouldn't happen with filters)\n\t\tif labName == \"\" {\n\t\t\tlabName = \"_unknown_lab_\"\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tgroupedDetails[labName] = append(groupedDetails[labName], containers[idx])\n\t}\n\n\tb, err := json.MarshalIndent(groupedDetails, \"\", \"  \")\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to marshal grouped container details: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tfmt.Println(string(b))\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc printContainerInspectJSON(contDetails []clabtypes.ContainerDetails) error {\n\t// Group summary results by LabName\n\t// Use a map where keys are lab names and values are slices of container details\n\tgroupedLabs := make(map[string][]clabtypes.ContainerDetails)\n\n\tfor idx := range contDetails {\n\t\tlabName := contDetails[idx].LabName\n\t\tif labName == \"\" {\n\t\t\tlabName = \"_unknown_lab_\" // Should not happen if filters work correctly\n\t\t}\n\t\t// Assign the *entire* ContainerDetails struct (including AbsLabPath)\n\t\tgroupedLabs[labName] = append(groupedLabs[labName], contDetails[idx])\n\t}\n\n\tb, err := json.MarshalIndent(groupedLabs, \"\", \"  \")\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to marshal grouped container summary: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tfmt.Println(string(b))\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc printContainerInspectTable(contDetails []clabtypes.ContainerDetails, o *Options) {\n\t// Generate and render table using the summary data (which uses relative LabPath)\n\ttabData := toTableData(contDetails, o)\n\ttable := tableWriter.NewWriter()\n\ttable.SetOutputMirror(os.Stdout)\n\ttable.SetStyle(tableWriter.StyleRounded)\n\ttable.Style().Format.Header = text.FormatTitle\n\ttable.Style().Format.HeaderAlign = text.AlignCenter\n\ttable.Style().Options.SeparateRows = true\n\ttable.Style().Color = tableWriter.ColorOptions{\n\t\tHeader: text.Colors{text.Bold},\n\t}\n\n\theaderBase := tableWriter.Row{\"Name\", \"Kind/Image\", \"State\", \"IPv4/6 Address\"}\n\tif o.Inspect.Wide {\n\t\theaderBase = slices.Insert(headerBase, 0, \"Owner\")\n\t}\n\n\tvar header tableWriter.Row\n\n\tvar colConfigs []tableWriter.ColumnConfig\n\n\t// Lab-level columns (Topology, Lab Name, Owner) AutoMerge across nodes of the same lab.\n\tif o.Destroy.All {\n\t\theader = append(tableWriter.Row{\"Topology\", \"Lab Name\"}, headerBase...)\n\t\tcolConfigs = append(\n\t\t\tcolConfigs,\n\t\t\ttableWriter.ColumnConfig{\n\t\t\t\tNumber:    1,\n\t\t\t\tAutoMerge: true, VAlign: text.VAlignMiddle,\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t\ttableWriter.ColumnConfig{\n\t\t\t\tNumber:    2, //nolint: mnd\n\t\t\t\tAutoMerge: true, VAlign: text.VAlignMiddle,\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t)\n\t\tif o.Inspect.Wide {\n\t\t\tcolConfigs = append(colConfigs, tableWriter.ColumnConfig{\n\t\t\t\tNumber:    3, //nolint: mnd\n\t\t\t\tAutoMerge: true, VAlign: text.VAlignMiddle,\n\t\t\t})\n\t\t}\n\t} else {\n\t\theader = headerBase\n\n\t\tcolConfigs = append(colConfigs, tableWriter.ColumnConfig{\n\t\t\tNumber:    1,\n\t\t\tAutoMerge: true, VAlign: text.VAlignMiddle,\n\t\t})\n\t}\n\n\ttable.AppendHeader(header)\n\n\tif len(colConfigs) > 0 {\n\t\ttable.SetColumnConfigs(colConfigs)\n\t}\n\n\ttable.AppendRows(tabData)\n\ttable.Render()\n}\n\nfunc printContainerInspectCSV(contDetails []clabtypes.ContainerDetails) {\n\tcsv := \"lab_name,labPath,absLabPath,name,container_id,image,kind,state,\" +\n\t\t\"status,ipv4_address,ipv6_address,owner\\n\"\n\tfor idx := range contDetails {\n\t\tcsv += fmt.Sprintf(\"%v,%v,%v,%v,%v,%v,%v,%v,%v,%v,%v,%v\\n\",\n\t\t\tcontDetails[idx].LabName,\n\t\t\tcontDetails[idx].LabPath,\n\t\t\tcontDetails[idx].AbsLabPath,\n\t\t\tcontDetails[idx].Name,\n\t\t\tcontDetails[idx].ContainerID,\n\t\t\tcontDetails[idx].Image,\n\t\t\tcontDetails[idx].Kind,\n\t\t\tcontDetails[idx].State,\n\t\t\tcontDetails[idx].Status,\n\t\t\tcontDetails[idx].IPv4Address,\n\t\t\tcontDetails[idx].IPv6Address,\n\t\t\tcontDetails[idx].Owner)\n\t}\n\n\tfmt.Print(csv)\n}\n\n// PrintContainerInspect handles non-details output (table or grouped JSON summary).\nfunc PrintContainerInspect(containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer, o *Options) error {\n\tcontDetails := make([]clabtypes.ContainerDetails, 0, len(containers))\n\n\t// Gather summary details of each container\n\tfor idx := range containers {\n\t\tabsPath := containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.TopoFile]\n\n\t\tshortPath, err := getShortestTopologyPath(absPath)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tlog.Warnf(\n\t\t\t\t\"failed to get relative topology path for container %s: %v, using raw path %q\",\n\t\t\t\tcontainers[idx].Names[0],\n\t\t\t\terr,\n\t\t\t\tabsPath,\n\t\t\t)\n\n\t\t\tshortPath = absPath // Use raw path as fallback for Reasoning
Now let's analyze the `logicalNodeContainers` function for correctness issues.

Key concerns:

1. **Ext-containers (external containers) without labels**: In `listContainers`, with `--topo`, `nodeContainers` from `ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound` may include ext-containers with no clab labels. In `logicalNodeContainers`, `key.node == ""` — for those, `key.node == ""` so `!isCPM` check: `key.node != "" && !isCPM` is false, so they pass through. Then `key.node = name` — name is `ctr.Labels[NodeName]` which is empty for ext-containers... Hmm, ext-containers: what labels do they have? Let's check. Ext-container kind: the container is external (e.g., a docker container started separately), and containerlab adds labels? Let me check the code. Actually in containerlab, ext-containers get labels injected when referenced? Let me check the source.

2. **Duplicate keys in `holders` map**: `holders[key] = &containers[idx]` — taking address of slice element; fine since range by index. But if multiple internal nodes for the same root, last wins. Fine.

3. **`isCPM` heuristic**: Components are named `<root node>-<lowercase slot>`. CPM A is slot "a", CPM B is slot "b". If a node has no CPM A but has CPM B, CPM B is considered the logical node. But what if a regular linux node is named `foo-b`? Then... wait, for regular nodes, `RootNodeName` label is empty, so `key.node == ""`, so the `key.node != "" && !isCPM` condition doesn't skip them. Good.

   But what about a regular (non-component) node whose name is e.g. `srl` with RootNodeName empty — fine.

   What about SR-SIM node "sr" whose components are "sr-1" (slot 1)? isCPM: name == "sr-a"? No. name == "sr-b" && !hasCPMA? hasCPMA true, so no. So `sr-1` is skipped. `sr-a` kept, `sr-b` skipped. Good.

   Edge: what if a node has a single CPM named slot B only? Then hasCPMA false, name == "sr-b" → isCPM true → kept as logical. That matches test.

   Edge: what about a component named e.g. "sr-a" being an MDA or XCM (line card)? XCM components are named `<node>-xcm-a`? Let's check srsim component naming. In containerlab srsim code, components are named like `sr-1`, `sr-a`, `sr-b`, and XCM might be `sr-xcm-1` or similar. Let me check the source.

4. **`hasCPMA` detection bug**: In the first loop, the case for `l[NodeName] == key.node+"-a"` sets hasCPMA. But this runs over all containers; for a container without root (key.node == ""), skipped. OK.

5. **Interaction with `o.Destroy.All` (inspect --all)**: previously `--all` showed internal containers (the base code only hid internal nodes when NOT `o.Destroy.All`). Now with this change, internal containers are always hidden in logical view; `--containers`/`--details` shows them. The docs updated. But wait — behavior change: previously `inspect --all` showed internal containers; now it doesn't. That's intentional per the test change ("Confirm inspect lists namespace holder only when containers are requested" — iterating selectors `--all` and `--name`, formats `-f json`, `--containers -f json`, `--details`; with `-f json` (no --containers) it should NOT contain netns; with --containers/--details it should). So intentional.

6. **Tool containers (sshx/gotty)**: previously shown? In base, internal nodes were hidden unless --all, but tool containers were shown in inspect. Now, `logicalNodeContainers` skips tool containers (`ToolType != ""`). So `inspect` (without --containers) no longer lists sshx/gotty tool containers. Is that intentional? The doc says "By default inspect shows one entry per topology node." Tool containers aren't topology nodes, so hiding them is intentional. But wait — does `ListContainers` with `WithListclabLabelExists` include tool containers? Yes. Previously they were shown (they have clab label but no lab name?). Hmm, actually tool containers get labels: clab=lab-name, tool-type=sshx. Previously they showed in inspect table. Now hidden by default. Probably intentional as part of "logical node" view. The changed test in 09-srsim.robot previously checked inspect `-a` includes holder; the new behavior documented.

   But there's a subtle issue: in deploy.go, `PrintContainerInspect` now filters out internal containers from deploy summary. Previously, the deploy summary did NOT filter internal containers (PrintContainerInspect filtered them when `!o.Destroy.All`; during deploy, `o.Destroy.All` is false, so actually deploy summary did filter them via PrintContainerInspect's check). Wait, the diff removes the filter inside PrintContainerInspect and moves it to deployFn. But listContainers is not used in deploy; deploy passes result.Containers directly. Previously PrintContainerInspect skipped internal containers when `!o.Destroy.All` — during deploy that's true, so they were filtered. Now filter moved to deployFn explicitly. 7. **Ext-containers in logicalNodeContainers**: ext-containers have labels? Check the ext-container kind. In containerlab, ext-container nodes are not created by containerlab; when listed via ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound, do they have `containerlab` label? I recall containerlab "claims" ext-containers by adding labels: `containerlab=<labname>`, `clab-node-name=<name>`, `containerlab=topo-file`? Let me check the source in the repo.

8. **Performance**: For --topo, `ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound` does a second full docker list; plus a dedup O(n²) ContainsFunc. Minor. Previously with --topo only one call. Now two calls. Docker list is cheap-ish. O(n²) with n = containers of one lab, small. Fine but might be P4.

Actually wait — there's a subtle behavior change with the dedup: nodeContainers (topology-based listing) may return containers in a different state; the merge keeps the first occurrence (label-based listing) and skips duplicates. OK.

9. **`logicalNodeContainers` holders map uses pointer to slice element** — `&containers[idx]`. This is fine.

10. **Bug: for a node where RootNodeName is set but NodeName label...** The internal netns holder has labels NodeName=<node>-netns and RootNodeName=<node>. The actual node container has NodeName=<node>, no RootNodeName? Wait, in the test: `ctr("lab1", "srl", "", false, "")` — the srl node itself has no RootNodeName label; `ctr("lab1", "srl-netns", "srl", true, ...)`. So key for srl: {lab1, ""} → key.node == "" → passes; then key.node = name = "srl". Then holders lookup: holders[{lab1, ""}]? holders map was filled only for key.node != ""... The holder had key {lab1, "srl"}, so `holders[{lab1,""}]` is nil. But the test expects srl to carry 10.0.0.2 (the holder IP). How does that work?

    Wait: order of processing. Containers: linux, srl, srl-netns, sr-1, sr-b, sr-a, sr-netns...

    Second loop: for `srl`: name = "srl", key = {lab1, ""}. isCPM computed: name == ""+"-a"? No. name == ""+"-b" && !hasCPMA[{lab1,""}]? No. So isCPM false. Condition: tooltype != "" false; internal false; (key.node != "" && !isCPM) → key.node == "" so false. So kept. key.node = "srl". holder = holders[{lab1,"srl"}] = &srl-netns. So NetworkSettings copied from holder. 

    So the map is keyed by {lab, root} where root = RootNodeName of the *holder*, and lookup uses key built from the node's labels where RootNodeName is empty → key.node = name after assignment. Clever: the lookup key is built before key.node is reassigned? Let's re-read:

    ```go
    ctr := containers[idx]
    name := ctr.Labels[NodeName]
    key := nodeKey{Containerlab, RootNodeName}
    isCPM := ...
    if ... continue
    if key.node == "" { key.node = name }
    if holder := holders[key]; holder != nil { ... }
    ```

    Yes — for the srl node, key = {lab1, ""}, then key.node = "srl", so lookup key is {lab1, "srl"}, matching holder registered under {lab1, "srl"}. Good.

    For the holder itself (internal, root "srl"), it's skipped in the result loop (internal == true → continue). Good.

    For sr: NodeName "sr"? In the test, `ctr("lab1", "sr-1", "sr", false, "")`, `ctr("lab1", "sr-b", "sr", false, "")`, `ctr("lab1", "sr-a", "sr", false, "")`. Hmm, where's the "sr" node itself? In SR-SIM distributed mode, the topology node is "sr" with components sr-a, sr-b, sr-1. There's no container named "sr" — the components are the containers. So logical node = "sr-a" (CPM A). If no CPM A, logical = "sr-b". The holder sr-netns is internal with root "sr". hasCPMA[{lab1,"sr"}] = true (from sr-a). sr-1: isCPM false → skip. sr-a: isCPM true (name == "sr-a") → keep, holder lookup {lab1,"sr"} → sr-netns → copy IPs. sr-b: isCPM false (hasCPMA true) → skip.

    lab2: sr-1 (root sr), sr-b (root sr), sr-netns. hasCPMA[{lab2,"sr"}] false. sr-1 skipped, sr-b kept (name=="sr-b" && !hasCPMA), holder lookup {lab2,"sr"} → sr-netns IP 10.0.0.4. Test expects "clab-lab2-sr-b 10.0.0.4". Good.

    So logic works for these cases.

843: 		return n.Cfg.Fqdn

845: 	fqdnDotIndex := strings.Index(n.Cfg.Fqdn, ".")

16: // ListContainers lists all containers using provided filter.
17: func (c *CLab) ListContainers(
18: 	ctx context.Context,
19: 	options ...ListOption,
20: ) ([]clabruntime.GenericContainer, error) {
21: 	opts := NewListOptions()
22: 
23: 	for _, opt := range options {
24: 		opt(opts)
25: 	}
26: 
27: 	var containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer
28: 
29: 	for _, r := range c.Runtimes {
30: 		ctrs, err := r.ListContainers(ctx, opts.ToFilters())
31: 		if err != nil {
32: 			return containers, fmt.Errorf("could not list containers: %v", err)
33: 		}
34: 
35: 		containers = append(containers, ctrs...)
36: 	}
37: 
38: 	return containers, nil
39: }

41: // ListNodesContainers lists all containers based on the nodes stored in clab instance.
42: func (c *CLab) ListNodesContainers(
43: 	ctx context.Context,
44: ) ([]clabruntime.GenericContainer, error) {
45: 	var containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer
46: 
47: 	for _, n := range c.Nodes {
48: 		cts, err := n.GetContainers(ctx)
49: 		if err != nil {
50: 			return containers, fmt.Errorf(
51: 				"could not get container for node %s: %v",
52: 				n.Config().LongName, err,
53: 			)
54: 		}

57: 	}
58: 
59: 	return containers, nil
60: }
61: 
62: // ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound lists all containers based on the nodes stored in clab
63: // instance, ignoring errors for non found containers.
64: func (c *CLab) ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound(
65: 	ctx context.Context,
66: ) ([]clabruntime.GenericContainer, error) {
67: 	var containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer
68: 
69: 	for _, n := range c.Nodes {
70: 		cts, err := n.GetContainers(ctx)
71: 		if err != nil {
72: 			continue
73: 		}
74: 
75: 		containers = append(containers, cts...)
76: 	}
77:

80: 
81: // ListContainerInterfaces list interfaces of the given container.
82: func (c *CLab) ListContainerInterfaces(
83: 	ctx context.Context,
84: 	container *clabruntime.GenericContainer,
85: ) (*clabtypes.ContainerInterfaces, error) {
86: 	containerInterfaces := clabtypes.ContainerInterfaces{}
87: 
88: 	if len(container.Names) > 0 {
89: 		containerInterfaces.ContainerName = container.Names[0]
90: 	}
91: 
92: 	// Retrieve the path to the container network NS
93: 	nodeNsPath, err := container.Runtime.GetNSPath(ctx, containerInterfaces.ContainerName)
94: 	if err != nil {
95: 		return nil, err
96: 	}
97: 
98: 	// Get network NS handle
99: 	var containerNsHandle netns.NsHandle
100: 
101: 	switch {
102: 	case nodeNsPath != "":
103: 		containerNsHandle, err = netns.GetFromPath(nodeNsPath)
104: 		if err != nil {
105: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf("unable to get container network NS handle: %w", err)
106: 		}
107: 		defer containerNsHandle.Close()
108: 	case container.Runtime.GetName() == "podman":
109: 		// Network NS path is empty and the runtime is Podman -> host network mode
110: 		// Manually get the handle for the root network namespace
111: 		containerNsHandle, err = netns.Get()
112: 		if err != nil {
113: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf("unable to get root network NS handle: %w", err)
114: 		}
115: 		defer containerNsHandle.Close()
116: 	default:
117: 		log.Warnf("Container %v has no namespace set, skipping!", containerInterfaces.ContainerName)
118: 		containerInterfaces.Interfaces = make([]*clabtypes.ContainerInterfaceDetails, 0)
119: 
120: 		return &containerInterfaces, nil
121: 	}
122: 
123: 	// Get Netlink handle in container network NS
124: 	netlinkHandle, err := netlink.NewHandleAt(containerNsHandle)
125: 	if err != nil {
126: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("unable to enter container network NS: %w", err)
127: 	}
128: 	defer netlinkHandle.Close()
129: 
130: 	interfaces, err := netlinkHandle.LinkList()
131: 	if err != nil {
132: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("unable to list network interfaces: %w", err)
133: 	}
134: 
135: 	containerInterfaces.Interfaces = make(
136: 		[]*clabtypes.ContainerInterfaceDetails,
137: 		0,
138: 		len(interfaces),
139: 	)
140: 
141: 	lab, node := TopoIdentity(container.Labels)
142: 
143: 	for _, iface := range interfaces {
144: 		reported, alias := reportedInterface(iface, lab, node)
145: 		attrs := reported.Attrs()
146: 
147: 		ifaceDetails := clabtypes.ContainerInterfaceDetails{
148: 			InterfaceName:  attrs.Name,
149: 			InterfaceAlias: alias,
150: 			InterfaceMTU:   attrs.MTU,
151: 			InterfaceMAC:   attrs.HardwareAddr.String(),
152: 			InterfaceIndex: attrs.Index,
153: 			InterfaceType:  reported.Type(),
154: 			InterfaceState: attrs.OperState.String(),
155: 		}
156: 		log.Debugf("Interface info: %+v", ifaceDetails)
157: 
158: 		containerInterfaces.Interfaces = append(containerInterfaces.Interfaces, &ifaceDetails)
159: 	}
160: 
161: 	log.Debugf("Fetched %v interfaces for %v", len(interfaces), containerInterfaces.ContainerName)
162: 
163: 	return &containerInterfaces, nil
164: }
165: 
166: // reportedInterface substitutes the host-side stitch interface for iface when
167: // one exists, keeping the topology name as alias so the swap is transparent.
168: func reportedInterface(iface netlink.Link, lab, node string) (netlink.Link, string) {
169: 	alias := iface.Attrs().Alias
170: 
171: 	topoName := alias
172: 	if topoName == "" {
173: 		topoName = iface.Attrs().Name
174: 	}
175: 
176: 	if tools, ok := clablinks.ToolsInterface(lab, node, topoName); ok {
177: 		return tools, topoName
178: 	}
179: 
180: 	return iface, alias
181: }
182: 
183: // ListContainersInterfaces list interfaces of all given containers.
184: func (c *CLab) ListContainersInterfaces(
185: 	ctx context.Context,
186: 	containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer,
187: ) ([]*clabtypes.ContainerInterfaces, error) {
188: 	containerInterfaces := make([]*clabtypes.ContainerInterfaces, 0, len(containers))
189: 
190: 	for idx := range containers {
191: 		cIfs, err := c.ListContainerInterfaces(ctx, &containers[idx])
192: 		if err != nil {
193: 			containerName := ""
194: 			if len(containers[idx].Names) > 0 {
195: 				containerName = containers[idx].Names[0]
196: 			}
197: 			log.Warn("Skipping interface detection", "node", containerName, "err", err)
198: 			continue
199: 		}
200: 
201: 		if len(cIfs.Interfaces) > 0 {
202: 			sort.Slice(cIfs.Interfaces, func(i, j int) bool {
203: 				return cIfs.Interfaces[i].InterfaceName < cIfs.Interfaces[j].InterfaceName
204: 			})
205: 		} else {
206: 			log.Warnf("No interfaces found for container %v", cIfs.ContainerName)
207: 		}
208: 
209: 		containerInterfaces = append(containerInterfaces, cIfs)
210: 	}
211: 
212: 	sort.Slice(containerInterfaces, func(i, j int) bool {
213: 		return containerInterfaces[i].ContainerName < containerInterfaces[j].ContainerName
214: 	})
215: 
216: 	return containerInterfaces, nil
217: }

17: var kindnames = []string{"ext-container"}
18: 
19: // Register registers the node in the NodeRegistry.
20: func Register(r *clabnodes.NodeRegistry) {
21: 	r.Register(kindnames, func() clabnodes.Node {
22: 		return new(extcont)
23: 	}, nil)
24: }
25: 
26: type extcont struct {
27: 	clabnodes.DefaultNode
28: }
29: 
30: func (s *extcont) Init(cfg *clabtypes.NodeConfig, opts ...clabnodes.NodeOption) error {
31: 	s.DefaultNode = *clabnodes.NewDefaultNode(s)
32: 	s.Cfg = cfg
33: 	for _, o := range opts {
34: 		o(s)
35: 	}
36: 	// Indicate that the pre-deployment UniquenessCheck is to be skipped.
37: 	// Since we would stop deployment on pre-existing containers.
38: 	s.Cfg.SkipUniquenessCheck = true
39: 	s.Cfg.LongName = s.Cfg.ShortName
40: 	return nil
41: }
42: 
43: func (e *extcont) Deploy(ctx context.Context, _ *clabnodes.DeployParams) error {
44: 	// check for the external dependency to be running
45: 	err := clabruntime.WaitForContainerRunning(ctx, e.Runtime, e.Cfg.ShortName, e.Cfg.ShortName)
46: 	if err != nil {
47: 		return err
48: 	}
49: 
50: 	e.SetState(clabnodesstate.Deployed)
51: 
52: 	return nil
53: }
54: 
55: // Delete we will not mess with external containers on delete.
56: func (*extcont) Delete(_ context.Context) error { return nil }
57: 
58: func (*extcont) Stop(context.Context) error { return nil }
59: 
60: func (e *extcont) Start(ctx context.Context) error {
61: 	return clabruntime.WaitForContainerRunning(ctx, e.Runtime, e.Cfg.ShortName, e.Cfg.ShortName)
62: }
63: 
64: // GetImages don't matter for external containers.
65: func (*extcont) GetImages(_ context.Context) map[string]string { return map[string]string{} }
66: func (*extcont) PullImage(_ context.Context) error             { return nil }
67: 
68: // GetContainerName returns the short name for the ext-container node, since for these nodes
69: // container name is specified in the topology file entirely.
70: func (e *extcont) GetContainerName() string {
71: 	return e.Cfg.ShortName
72: }
73: 
74: func (e *extcont) GetContainers(ctx context.Context) ([]clabruntime.GenericContainer, error) {
75: 	containers, err := e.DefaultNode.GetContainers(ctx)
76: 	if err != nil {
77: 		return nil, err
78: 	}
79: 
80: 	// we need to artificially add the Node Kind Label
81: 	// this label data is e.g. used in the table printed after deployment
82: 	for idx := range containers {
83: 		containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.NodeKind] = kindnames[0]
84: 	}
85: 	return containers, nil

9: 	clabutils "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/utils"

12: 	clabtypes "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/types"
13: 	"github.com/vishvananda/netns"
14: )
15: 
16: func (n *sros) newNetnsConfig() *clabtypes.NodeConfig {
17: 	labels := maps.Clone(n.Cfg.Labels)
18: 
19: 	if labels == nil {
20: 		labels = map[string]string{}
21: 	}
22: 
23: 	shortName := fmt.Sprintf("%s-%s", n.Cfg.ShortName, "netns")
24: 	labels[clabconstants.NodeName] = shortName
25: 
26: 	longName := fmt.Sprintf("%s-%s", n.Cfg.LongName, "netns")
27: 	labels[clabconstants.LongName] = longName
28: 
29: 	labels[clabconstants.NodeType] = "srsim-netns"
30: 	labels[clabconstants.InternalNode] = "true"
31: 
32: 	// this node will be the root node
33: 	labels[clabconstants.RootNodeName] = n.Cfg.ShortName
34: 	labels[clabconstants.RootNodeLongName] = n.Cfg.LongName
35: 
36: 	return &clabtypes.NodeConfig{
37: 		ShortName:             shortName,
38: 		LongName:              longName,
39: 		LabDir:                n.Cfg.LabDir,
40: 		Index:                 n.Cfg.Index,
41: 		Kind:                  n.Cfg.Kind,
42: 		NodeType:              "srsim-netns",
43: 		Image:                 n.Cfg.Image,
44: 		ImagePullPolicy:       n.Cfg.ImagePullPolicy,
45: 		RestartPolicy:         n.Cfg.RestartPolicy,
46: 		Sysctls:               maps.Clone(n.Cfg.Sysctls),
47: 		User:                  "0:0",
48: 		Entrypoint:            "/bin/sh",
49: 		Cmd:                   `-c "trap 'exit 0' TERM INT; sleep infinity & wait $!"`,
50: 		Env:                   map[string]string{},
51: 		PortBindings:          n.Cfg.PortBindings,
52: 		PortSet:               n.Cfg.PortSet,
53: 		NetworkMode:           n.Cfg.NetworkMode,
54: 		MgmtNet:               n.Cfg.MgmtNet,

58: 		MgmtIPv4PrefixLength:  n.Cfg.MgmtIPv4PrefixLength,
59: 		MgmtIPv6Address:       n.Cfg.MgmtIPv6Address,
60: 		MgmtIPv6AddressAuto:   n.Cfg.MgmtIPv6AddressAuto,
61: 		MgmtIPv6PrefixLength:  n.Cfg.MgmtIPv6PrefixLength,
62: 		MgmtIPv4Gateway:       n.Cfg.MgmtIPv4Gateway,
63: 		MgmtIPv6Gateway:       n.Cfg.MgmtIPv6Gateway,
64: 		MacAddress:            n.Cfg.MacAddress,
65: 		Aliases:               n.Cfg.Aliases,
66: 		DNS:                   n.Cfg.DNS,
67: 		ExtraHosts:            n.Cfg.ExtraHosts,
68: 		Labels:                labels,
69: 		Runtime:               n.Cfg.Runtime,
70: 		ResultingPortBindings: n.Cfg.ResultingPortBindings,
71: 	}
72: }
73: 
74: func (n *sros) cleanupParkingNetNS() error {
75: 	name := clabutils.ParkingNetnsName(n.Cfg.LongName)
76: 	if _, err := clabutils.GetNamedNetNS(name); err != nil {
77: 		return nil
78: 	}
79: 
80: 	return netns.DeleteNamed(name)
81: }
82: 
83: // GetNSPath retrieves the Namespace Path.
84: func (n *sros) GetNSPath(ctx context.Context) (string, error) {
85: 	if n.isStandaloneNode() || (n.isDistributedCardNode() && n.rootCtrName == "") {
86: 		return n.DefaultNode.GetNSPath(ctx)
87: 	} else if n.isDistributedCardNode() {
88: 		return n.Runtime.GetNSPath(ctx, n.rootCtrName)
89: 	}
90: 	return n.netnsNode.GetNSPath(ctx)
91: }
92: 
93: // DeleteNetnsSymlink deletes the symlink file created for the container netns.
94: func (n *sros) DeleteNetnsSymlink() error {
95: 	var errs []error
96: 
97: 	// if it is the base node, then we need to delete the symlink for all the components.
98: 	if n.isDistributedBaseNode() {
99: 		for _, componentNode := range n.componentNodes {
100: 			errs = append(errs, componentNode.DeleteNetnsSymlink())
101: 		}
102: 		if n.netnsNode != nil {
103: 			errs = append(errs, n.netnsNode.DeleteNetnsSymlink())
104: 		}
105: 		errs = append(errs, clabutils.DeleteNetnsSymlink(n.Cfg.LongName))
106: 	}
107: 
108: 	errs = append(errs, n.DefaultNode.DeleteNetnsSymlink(), n.cleanupParkingNetNS())
109: 
110: 	return errors.Join(errs...)
111: }

17: // usesNetnsNode reports whether the node runs in the network namespace of a holder container.
18: // Nodes with a user-defined network mode keep the network namespace of their own container.
19: func (n *srl) usesNetnsNode() bool {
20: 	return n.Cfg.NetworkMode == "" && !n.Cfg.IsRootNamespaceBased
21: }
22: 
23: func (n *srl) initNetnsNode() error {
24: 	if !n.usesNetnsNode() {
25: 		return nil
26: 	}
27: 
28: 	n.netnsNode = new(clabnodes.NetnsHolder)
29: 
30: 	return n.netnsNode.Init(n.newNetnsConfig(), clabnodes.WithRuntime(n.GetRuntime()))
31: }

33: func (n *srl) newNetnsConfig() *clabtypes.NodeConfig {

91: // containerConfig returns the config of the SR Linux container attached to the holder's
92: // network namespace. The runtime rejects network settings for such containers, as they
93: // belong to the holder.
94: func (n *srl) containerConfig() *clabtypes.NodeConfig {
95: 	cfg := *n.Cfg
96: 	cfg.NetworkMode = "container:" + n.netnsNode.GetShortName()
97: 	cfg.DNS = nil
98: 	cfg.ExtraHosts = nil
99: 	cfg.MacAddress = ""
100: 	cfg.PortBindings = nil
101: 	cfg.PortSet = nil
102:

105: 
106: // linkNetns points the node's netns symlink to the holder, as the namespace path of the
107: // SR Linux container changes whenever the container restarts.
108: func (n *srl) linkNetns(ctx context.Context) error {
109: 	nsPath, err := n.netnsNode.GetNSPath(ctx)
110: 	if err != nil {
111: 		return err
112: 	}
113: 
114: 	return clabutils.LinkContainerNS(nsPath, n.Cfg.LongName)
115: }
116: 
117: // deployedWithoutNetnsNode reports whether the node's container runs without a holder: it keeps
118: // its own network namespace, as a node deployed by a containerlab before the holder does until
119: // it is recreated.
120: func (n *srl) deployedWithoutNetnsNode(ctx context.Context) bool {
121: 	return n.netnsNode.GetContainerStatus(ctx) == clabruntime.NotFound &&
122: 		n.GetContainerStatus(ctx) != clabruntime.NotFound
123: }
124: 
125: // GetNSPath retrieves the Namespace Path.
126: func (n *srl) GetNSPath(ctx context.Context) (string, error) {
127: 	if n.netnsNode == nil || n.deployedWithoutNetnsNode(ctx) {
128: 		return n.DefaultNode.GetNSPath(ctx)
129: 	}
130: 
131: 	return n.netnsNode.GetNSPath(ctx)
132: }
133:

136: 	if n.netnsNode == nil {
137: 		return n.DefaultNode.DeleteNetnsSymlink()
138: 	}
139:

Reasoning
`c.deploy(ctx, options)` returns containers. Let me find where containers is built in `c.deploy` — probably `c.ListNodesContainers` or per-node aggregation. In reconcile path, it's `c.ListNodesContainers(ctx)` which iterates c.Nodes — for a distributed sros node, `n.GetContainers` returns the CPM A container (cnts[0] with forged IP) — only ONE container per topology node! So ListNodesContainers returns one container per node (CPM A for distributed sros). That explains the deploy summary expecting "linux sr-a".

For fresh deploy path, let me check what `c.deploy` returns for containers.

<content>
1: ---
2: tags:
3:   - Command
4: ---
5: 
6: # Inspect command
7: 
8: ### Description
9: 
10: The `inspect` command provides the information about the deployed labs.
11: 
12: /// note | Clabernetes runtime
13: With `--runtime c9s`, `inspect` reads Clabernetes `Topology` resources and their related kubernetes objects instead of local Docker containers. `inspect --all` lists c9s topologies across all namespaces, or in the `--namespace` override. The JSON output uses `<namespace>/<lab>/<node>` as `container_id` and adds the `namespace`, `pod`, `container`, `node_uid`, `exposed_ports` and `load_balancer_address` fields. See [Containerlab runtime](../../manual/clabernetes/runtime.md#inspect) for details.
14: ///
15: 
16: ### Usage
17: 
18: `containerlab [global-flags] inspect [local-flags]`
19: 
20: ### Flags
21: 
22: #### all
23: 
24: With the local `--all` flag it's possible to list all deployed labs in a single table. The output will also show the relative path to the topology file that was used to spawn this lab.
25: 
26: The lab name and path values will be set for the first node of such lab, to reduce the clutter. Refer to the [examples](#examples) section for more details.
27: 
28: #### topology | name
29: 
30: With the global `--topo | -t` flag a user sets the path to the topology definition file that will be used to spin up a lab.
31: 
32: When the topology path refers to a directory, containerlab will look for a file with `.clab.yml` extension in that directory and use it as a topology definition file.
33: 
34: When the topology file flag is omitted, containerlab will try to find the matching file name by looking at the current working directory.
35: 
36: If more than one file is found for directory-based path or when the flag is omitted entirely, containerlab will fail with an error.
37: 
38: #### format
39: 
40: The local `--format` flag enables different output stylings. By default the table view will be used.
41: 
42: Available format options are:
43: 
44: - `json` - outputs the inspect data in JSON format
45: - `csv` - outputs the inspect data as comma-separated values.
46: 
47: The data is provided in the context of a container that belongs to a containerlab lab. Both output formats will provide you with the following data:
48: 
49: | Field          | Description                                                                                              |
50: |----------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
51: | `lab_name`     | The name of the lab.                                                                                     |
52: | `labPath`      | The relative path to the lab topology file.                                                              |
53: | `absLabPath`   | The absolute path to the lab topology file.                                                              |
54: | `name`         | The name of the container.                                                                               |
55: | `container_id` | The unique ID of the container.                                                                          |
56: | `image`        | The container's [image](../../manual/nodes.md#image).                                                    |
57: | `kind`         | The container's [kind](../../manual/nodes.md#kind).                                                      |
58: | `state`        | The container's state. ie. `created`, `restarting`, `running`, `removing`, `paused`, `exited`, or `dead` |
59: | `status`       | The health status of the container. ie `starting`, `unhealthy`, `up` or `exited`.                        |
60: | `ipv4_address` | The [IPv4 management address](../../manual/nodes.md#mgmt-ipv4) of the container.                            |
61: | `ipv6_address` | The [IPv6 management address](../../manual/nodes.md#mgmt-ipv6) of the container.                            |
62: | `owner`        | The [owner](../deploy.md#owner) of the lab.                                                                 |
63: 
64: #### details
65: 
66: The `inspect` command produces a brief summary about the running lab components. It is also possible to get a full view on the running containers by adding `--details` flag.
67: 
68: With this flag inspect command will output every bit of information about the running containers. This is what `docker inspect` command provides.
69: 
70: #### containers
71: 
72: By default `inspect` shows one entry per topology node.
73: 
74: Nodes that consist of several containers (ie. distributed SR-SIM) are represented by a single node that carries the management addresses of the node.
75: 
76: The local flag `--containers` will list every container of a lab rather than just the logical nodes.
77: 
78: When the lab is selected with the `--topo` flag, only the containers of the nodes defined in that topology file are listed.
79: 
80: #### wide
81: 
82: The local `-w | --wide` flag adds all available columns to the `inspect` output table.
83: 
84: ### Examples
85: 
86: #### List all running labs on the host
87: 
88: ```bash
89: ❯ containerlab inspect --all
90: +---+------------+----------+-----------------+--------------+--------------------+------+-------+---------+----------------+----------------------+
91: | # | Topo Path  | Lab Name |      Name       | Container ID |       Image        | Kind | Group |  State  |  IPv4 Address  |     IPv6 Address     |
92: +---+------------+----------+-----------------+--------------+--------------------+------+-------+---------+----------------+----------------------+
93: | 1 | newlab.yml | newlab   | clab-newlab-n1  | 3c8262034088 | srlinux:20.6.3-145 | srl  |       | running | 172.20.20.4/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::4/80 |
94: | 2 |            |          | clab-newlab-n2  | 79c562b71997 | srlinux:20.6.3-145 | srl  |       | running | 172.20.20.5/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::5/80 |
95: | 3 | srl02.yml  | srl01    | clab-srl01-srl  | 13c9e7543771 | srlinux:20.6.3-145 | srl  |       | running | 172.20.20.2/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::2/80 |
96: | 4 |            |          | clab-srl01-srl2 | 8cfca93b7b6f | srlinux:20.6.3-145 | srl  |       | running | 172.20.20.3/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::3/80 |
97: +---+------------+----------+-----------------+--------------+--------------------+------+-------+---------+----------------+----------------------+
98: ```
99: 
100: #### Provide information about a specific running lab by its name
101:

The JSON output uses `<namespace>/<lab>/<node>` as `container_id` and adds the `namespace`, `pod`, `container`, `node_uid`, `exposed_ports` and `load_balancer_address` fields. See [Containerlab runtime](../../manual/clabernetes/runtime.md#inspect) for details.\n///\n\n### Usage\n\n`containerlab [global-flags] inspect [local-flags]`\n\n### Flags\n\n#### all\n\nWith the local `--all` flag it's possible to list all deployed labs in a single table. By default the table view will be used.\n\nAvailable format options are:\n\n- `json` - outputs the inspect data in JSON format\n- `csv` - outputs the inspect data as comma-separated values.\n\nThe data is provided in the context of a container that belongs to a containerlab lab.
